7 Signs Your Therapist Might Not Be a Good Fit for You

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 9, 2026 | Reviewed by Davia Sills

Finding the right therapist can feel a lot like dating. You browse through profiles, ask around, and then schedule an initial meeting, hoping there’s chemistry. Sometimes you click immediately, but other times, you have to wait for your therapist to grow on you.

Just like any other relationship, the therapeutic relationship is about finding the right match. Not every therapist is going to be a good fit for you. Just because someone has a degree, a cute office, and a comfy couch doesn’t necessarily mean they have the capacity to support your needs.

Therapy is an investment of your time, money, and emotional energy. You deserve to feel that the person sitting across from you is genuinely invested in understanding you and helping you grow.

Of course, good therapy isn’t always comfortable. In fact, the most meaningful sessions are often the ones that leave you a little confused and a little raw. Questioning your assumptions, confronting your beliefs, and recognizing patterns are non-negotiable aspects of successful therapy.

But there’s a difference between being challenged in therapy and feeling that your therapist isn’t meeting your needs.

Here are seven signs your therapist might not be the right fit.

If your therapist thinks you’re always right, all your exes are narcissists, and everyone in your family is toxic, you may have hired a very expensive cheerleader.

A good therapist will validate your feelings, but they’ll also, in the nicest possible way, confront you and challenge your assumptions.

Ask yourself if your therapist invites you to consider another perspective and helps you confront how your own fears, expectations, and behaviors contribute to your experiences rather than just validating you.

You’ve spent three sessions talking about how your sister wasn’t invited to your wedding because she had an affair with your fiancé. And the next week, your therapist asks, “So, how are things with your sister? Have you two been spending time together?”

Therapists don’t need photographic memories. We’re human, and we talk to a lot of people. But it’s our responsibility to find ways to remember important patterns, relationships, and events in someone’s life so we can help them build awareness and make connections.

When a therapist repeatedly forgets significant details, you may find yourself feeling betrayed and spending session time reiterating the same stories. Also, paying attention to your story is a part of helping you feel seen. It’s challenging to develop trust when you feel dismissed, which is inevitable when someone who is paid to pay attention to you doesn't.

You chat, you laugh, you catch up, but the conversations don’t lead anywhere.

You discuss your weekend, complain about your boss, recap your latest dating disaster, and suddenly your 50 minutes are up. See you next Tuesday!

Therapy isn’t always about measurable progress, and chitchat is part of relationship building, but there should be some sense of direction, insight, and exploration that your sessions build on.

Seemingly ordinary conversations can allow us to make significant discoveries. A conversation about a disagreement with a roommate might open the door to exploring your discomfort with conflict or your tendency to prioritize everyone else’s needs.

You don’t need a life-changing revelation every week, but over time, therapy should offer more than an opportunity to vent.

Your mother is histrionic. Your dad is a workaholic. Your boyfriend is avoidant. Apparently, you’re the only psychologically healthy person in your world.

A good therapist helps you understand relational dynamics, identify your own role in them, and empowers you to make changes, rather than handing out diagnoses to people they’ve never met.

It’s tempting to explain difficult relationships by attaching psychological labels to the people involved. However, your therapist’s job is to recognize problematic behaviors, not assign a clinical diagnosis based on someone else’s account.

The goal of therapy should be to guide you to understand your relationships, empower you, and help you develop healthier ways of navigating them.

You’re reaching for tissues as you tell your therapist about a painful breakup, and suddenly you’re hearing a 15-minute story about their divorce.

Appropriate self-disclosure can be useful, but if you’re learning more about your therapist than yourself, something isn’t right.

Therapists are people, too. We have families, hobbies, favorite books, and experiences that sometimes overlap with those of our clients. Sharing a personal anecdote can help normalize an experience, strengthen connection, or reduce isolation. However, if you regularly find yourself listening to your therapist’s problems, comforting them, or wondering how to redirect the conversation back to yourself, the boundaries may have become blurred.

You tell them something they said didn’t sit right with you, and suddenly they’re more defensive than your ex.

A good therapist can acknowledge mistakes, welcome feedback, and work through ruptures in the therapeutic relationship.

Your therapist, who is also human, may make inaccurate assumptions, forget a session, or approach a sensitive topic a little too abruptly. Human error is inevitable in any meaningful relationship, including therapy. Misunderstandings in therapy may serve as an opportunity for you to voice your needs and boundaries. If your therapist is able to respond with accountability, that might serve as a corrective experience and may even strengthen the therapeutic alliance.

In contrast, a therapist who becomes dismissive, defensive, or makes you feel guilty for expressing your concerns may unintentionally reinforce the very relational patterns you’re trying to change.

A good therapist isn’t there to make your decisions for you. They should help you understand what’s driving your choices, explore your options, and develop the confidence to make decisions that are right for you.

Of course, therapists can offer guidance, practical suggestions, and recommendations when appropriate. Therapy is about helping someone evaluate their choices, not telling them how to live.

For example, you might already know that your boyfriend is letting you down. What you may not understand is why leaving him feels impossible or why being alone feels more frightening than being with someone who is not all that nice to you. Good therapy helps you develop the insight and emotional capacity to make your own decisions, even when those decisions are difficult.

Your therapist doesn’t need to be your best friend, your personal cheerleader, or your life coach. They should be someone who helps you see what you might not want to see while creating an environment where you feel safe enough to explore it.

And remember, a therapist can be perfectly competent and still not be the right fit for you.

Different therapists have different personalities, approaches, and areas of expertise. Some are more structured and directive, while others are more exploratory. Some employ humor and use themselves as a part of the process, while others are a little more distant and leave more room for reflection. What works beautifully for one person may feel unhelpful to another.

Many therapists offer complimentary consultation sessions for you to ask them questions and assess fit. Don’t feel like you have to commit to the first therapist you meet.

Therapy asks you to be vulnerable, honest, and willing to examine parts of yourself you may have spent years avoiding. Finding someone you can trust with that process is an important piece.

To find a therapist near you, visit the Psychology Today Therapy Directory.

The game has been over for months, but shots are still being fired.

We're well past Big Brother Season 28's finale on the 2026 TV schedule, but former Houseguests are still taking shots at each other. Runner-up Taylor Brown has had a chance to watch some episodes, and as a result, she was able to see what Melody Morris had to say. With that, Taylor is now wondering how Melody had any right to discuss strategy.

In a recent livestream featuring fellow Houseguest LaTrice "LaLa" Verrett and one of BB's most memorable winners, Jun Song, Taylor went off. Brown mentioned Melody's critique against her and how she supported the narrative that the latter "did nothing" in the game. On top of all that, Taylor also wanted to know what made Melody any different:

What do you do? I would say we're the same person, different fonts! Because if you asked me, anytime I was in the pod I looked in the mirror and you were under me! She was in the bed just as much as me...if the perception is just I'm cooking and cleaning, what are you doing?

She makes a good point, but it's also one that many other Big Brother fans have been making. Those outside the house were equally frustrated that Melody hadn't done anything in the game, even when her closest allies were in jeopardy. Meanwhile, she was hanging out with members of F.A.P. and The Crossovers and talking about how Taylor did nothing in the game.

Dee's Advice For Future Big Brother Houseguests Is Exactly What The Series Needs

As for why Taylor is only bringing this up now, it always takes a while for Big Brother's late-game Houseguests to catch up with what others were saying during the season. They obviously couldn't watch the live feeds with a Paramount+ subscription, and sometimes the television edit is deceiving.

Given that, I think Taylor has finally had the time to check out some of the episodes and see some of the live feed clips sent to her by fans. This probably prompted the response about Melody, who truly didn't have a better chance of winning Big Brother than Taylor did, in my opinion.

When talking about her own game, Taylor has acknowledged her shortcomings. She admitted that the finale Q&A was not her finest moment and that she choked when it came to answering the big questions. She also called out Angela Murray and others for their reactions to her questions, which Melody also played a part in.

As for Melody, she hasn't mentioned Taylor a that much in the past few weeks. Primarily, she's been on a tour around the United States with Drew Campbell, and simultaneously continues to tease fans about whether or not they're dating. I would be curious to see what Melody has to say about all of this, and if there's any mutual beef on her end with Taylor putting her on blast.

Kobe Bryant No. 8 Lakers jersey fetches huge money at auction

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Mamba Mentality continues to draw attention.

A game-worn No. 8 Kobe Bryant jersey from the 2003-2004 season sold for $1.83 million at The Realest on Sunday, Nov. 8. This is the highest sale of a non-rookie jersey from the Los Angeles Lakers star before he switched to No. 24. It also lands among the top-five highest public auction sales of Bryant's NBA jerseys.

The jersey is autographed and was certified by The Realest's authentication program, which utilizes photo-matching. Its only previous owner purchased the jersey with a certificate of authenticity directly from the Lakers via a charity auction in 2017 before the NBA icon passed away.

The jersey is believed to have been worn in 17 games that season – with a record of 16-1, including 8-0 in the playoffs – when the five-time champion starred alongside Shaquille O'Neal. Bryant wore it for his 31-point, 10-assist double-double in Game 5 of the first round to eliminate the Houston Rockets, for his 42-point performance in Game 4 of the conference semifinals against the San Antonio Spurs and in Game 6 of the next series against the top-seeded Minnesota Timberwolves to win the Western Conference finals.

The jersey has an NBA Finals patch and is the only No. 8 jersey of Bryant's to come to auction with such a detail. It could not be proven if Bryant wore it the NBA Finals, where the Lakers lost to the Detroit Pistons in five games.

The highest-selling Bryant jersey was the one from his NBA debut in 1996. It sold in April of 2025 for $7 million.

Bryant played 20 seasons in the NBA all with the Lakers. He was drafted No. 13 overall in the 1996 NBA Draft by the Charlotte Hornets out of Lower Merion High School in Philadelphia and was traded to Los Angeles that day. He won five NBA championships with the Lakers, was named Finals MVP for two of the titles and was named an All-Star 18 times.

Bryant died in a helicopter crash in January of 2020 at the age of 41 with his 13-year-old daughter, Gianna.

Last month, The Realest auctioned off a Shohei Ohtani jersey from the 2025 Tokyo Series for $2,055,700. It was the most expensive jersey worn by a baseball player in the 21st century.

The biggest stories, every morning. Stay up-to-date on all the key sports developments by subscribing to USA TODAY Sports' newsletter.

7 Alan Ritchson Movies & TV Shows Reacher Fans Will Love

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Faith Roswell is a Senior Writer on Screen Rant's Classic TV team. Since earning her degree in Creative Writing over a decade ago, Faith has written articles on film and TV from a variety of different angles. Faith now combines her knowledge of psychology with her love of monster movies to give more insight into what makes the best ones.  You may have read her Screen Rant lists and features covering horror, sci-fi, and fantasy, or read her Amazon Top 10 book, "Movie Monsters of the Deep." Faith has had an extensive career as a writer, appearing on BBC live radio, researching true crime for Rotten Mango podcast, and writing for publications including Mental Floss, Atlas Obscura, and The Daily Jaws before beginning here at Screen Rant.

When Reacher first aired, it appeared to many that Alan Ritchson exploded onto the screen and has barely left since, but while Reacher might be Ritchson's best TV show, his onscreen career began 20 years ago. His early roles made use of his distinctive height and physique, with the younger actor playing Aquaman in the superhero TV series Smallville, Gloss in The Hunger Games franchise, and having his first major role as the linebacker Kevin "Thad" Castle in the TV sitcom Blue Mountain State. However, Reacher then became a gamechanger for the actor.

Reacher instantly drew attention for featuring a more faithful portrayal of Lee Child's iconic character than in Tom Cruise's movies, but the show made some significant changes from the books, allowing Ritchson more versatility. Despite being somewhat typecast, Prime Video's best action series gave him the role that he was born to play, making him into one of the decade's biggest action stars and highlighting his other movies and TV shows both before and since that are must-watches for Reacher fans.

Syfy's gritty, ridiculous sci-fi action TV series is Fallout meets Mad Max, with a side of Death Race. In the dystopian future of an alternate 1999, gasoline and water are ruinously expensive and a mysterious corporation oversees virtually every aspect of daily life. Ritchson plays Los Angeles police officer Arthur "Barbie" Bailey, who is forced to enter the titular "Blood Drive," a race in which the cars run on human blood. The show, which is as gory as its description suggests, was canceled after one season, but it has a surprising 86% positive audience score on Rotten Tomatoes.

A few scenes in season 4 saw Reacher becoming a horror show, but it is firmly grounded in the action genre. That said, the series has become known for its improbable and often hilarious fight scenes, which see Reacher using virtually any object in reach to deal devastating impact. Fans who love this aspect of the series and enjoy the Mad Max franchise should not miss Blood Drive for more of the same, but with even wilder twists.

After playing Aquaman in Smallville and voicing Raphael in the Teenage Mutant Ninja Turtles movie, Ritchson plays his third superhero role in HBO's 49-episode action series, Titans. Ritchson appeared as Hank Hall, aka Hawk, in the first season of the show, with his role being expanded for the second and third seasons. With the show being based on the DC Comics Teen Titans characters, it may not come as a surprise that its target audience skews younger than Reacher's, but the series is well worth watching.

One of Alan Ritchson's most unusual roles has been his work as the body model for Beowulf in the 2007 movie of the same name.

Titans might be aimed at slightly younger viewers but it is by no means a kids' show, and essential viewing for fans of the DC Universe. It is violent, intense, and with Reacher estimated to return in 2027, this leaves more than enough time to start the series. Ritchson gets to play a vigilante with plenty of opportunity to beat up bad guys, but as part of a team rather than carrying the entire series. Titans also stars Titus Welliver (Bosch,) Anna Diop (Us,) and Esai Morales (How to Get Away with Murder.)

The 2026 action comedy movie Runner sees Ritchson starring along Owen Wilson in a race against time, as the two must make an important delivery while being pursued by dangerous enemies. Reacher has cemented Alan Ritchson firmly within a certain type of role, and he plays it very well. In Runner's case, he is a former Special Forces operative, who prefers to keep his own company and has strained relationships with people from his past. Working as a transporter of legal and illegal items, this assignment sees him in charge of a human liver.

With the liver intended for transplant and the cartel in pursuit, the movie is fast-paced, often hilarious, and features a very similar dynamic to the Reacher series, with Ritchson's character acting as the protector and initially reluctant friend to a physically weaker character with skills of his own. Reacher fans will enjoy Runner for its impressive stunts and old-school action tone, and while it was less well-received with Rotten Tomatoes critics, who gave the movie a still-solid 78% positive score, audiences rated it 89%.

Motor City might have received middling Rotten Tomatoes ratings from both critics and audiences on its initial release, but Alan Ritchson's gritty revenge thriller has conquered streaming instead. The movie, which also stars Shailene Woodley (Divergent) and Lionel Boyce (The Bear,) follows John Miller (Ritchson,) who breaks out of prison after being framed, and sets off on a revenge mission against the gangster who framed him and stole his girlfriend.

Aside from being action-heavy, Motor City has a surprising detail in common with Reacher, as both Ritchson's characters rarely speak. This is one of the defining features of Jack Reacher as imagined by Lee Child, but the series made some necessary changes, giving the character more dialogue to replace the books series' interior monologues. People who wanted to see a more accurate version of Reacher in this respect should watch Motor City, which features almost no dialogue at all.

The moments in which Reacher takes down an impressive line-up of opponents single-handedly are some of the best in the series, but the few scenes that show him as part of a group are some of the most heartwarming. With Reacher being a drifter who avoids relationships, the series rarely offers scenes like the ones in season 2 that introduce the dynamic within the 110th Special Investigators, but The Ministry of Ungentlemanly Warfare has plenty. Based on the book of the same name, the movie follows the first-ever Special Forces organization, comprized of a team of misfits using unconventional strategies.

The Ministry of Ungentlemanly Warfare became Netflix's top movie in September, and its stacked cast and genre mix have earned it a 91% positive audience rating on Rotten Tomatoes. Ritchson plays Major Anders Larssen, the glasses-wearing weapons expert whose skill with a bow and arrow reflects the abilities of his real-life inspiration. Like Reacher, Larssen is a man of few words, but Ritchson still trained with a dialect coach to perfect his Danish accent, and described Larssen as "the baddest dude I’ve ever seen on paper in my life" in an interview with The Hollywood Reporter.

The reaction to the announcement of a Neagley-centered spinoff was mixed, with people who loved Maria Sten's portrayal of the iconic character excited to see more of her, while others were concerned that Neagley might not be able to carry an entire show alone. These concerns were quickly laid to rest, with Neagley being a fantastic action series even without Reacher's constant presence, and a perfect replacement for Reacher as we wait for the next season. Unlike most of the movies and TV shows on this list, Alan Ritchson is barely seen, but this was an excellent creative decision.

Neagley was allowed to develop as a character and series without living in Reacher's shadow, so by the time Ritchson reprised his career-defining role, Neagley had already claimed a place among Prime Video's greatest action TV shows. With Neagley renewed for a second season, it remains to be seen how much of a role Ritchson will play, but many fans of both series hope for a few more crossovers, with their dynamic bringing out the best in both characters and actors.

War Machine is as close to a sci-fi Reacher movie as it is possible to get. Instead of insidious government conspiracies and smaller scale villains terrorizing their towns, Ritchson's nameless character is up against a far greater threat. The Reacher star's R-rated thriller sees with a gruelling military training excercise turn into a fight for survival against a machine that starts targeting and killing the team, resulting in reviewers comparing the movie to Predator, Terminator, and War of the Worlds.

Known only as 81, Ritchson plays a Staff Sergeant on a mission to pass training and prove himself as a Ranger, but while he is physically one of the strongest people in the group, he does not bond with them, preferring to work alone until the threat from the machine drastically changes the team's dynamic. The movie received middling Rotten Tomatoes scores, but it is a lot of fun, especially for fans of both Reacher and sci-fi, and a sequel is now reportedly in progress.

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

The best of ScreenRant, directly in your inbox.

Jérôme Rothen pays Pierre Sage

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Fifteenth in the Premier League last season, Crystal Palace took the gamble of enlisting Pierre Sage as their new coach. The former Lens coach chose to only stay one year in Artois before giving in to the English sirens. But for the moment, Sage’s British adventure is not going very well. Seventeenth in the rankings, the Eagles are struggling to take off and the 47-year-old French technician has already annoyed his president. For Jérôme Rothen, Sage pays for a bad attitude.

“He said everything and its opposite last season. (…) In his attitude, he saw himself as too beautiful. Congratulations for the victory in the Coupe de France with Lens. (…) What’s funny today is that he criticizes Crystal Palace that recruitment is not up to what he expected, but on the other hand in Lens, it didn’t bother him to say nothing about recruitment because he hadn’t participated in it. (…) We gave him players, he made this mayonnaise very well internally. But since we had to pull the cover for him, because for me it’s a false modesty, he said: “the year in Lens, magnificent.” And there, a big English club comes to pick him up. That’s when he saw himself looking too good. If you are sure of your strength, you do things differently with your leaders in Lens and you would have agreed to play the Champions League with Lens. But given that he lacked experience and knowledge of high-level football, the first club made him an offer and there they explained to us that he could not refuse Crystal Palace. But Crystal Palace, last year, they finished 15th or 16th. (…) What we see is that in the first 12 matches, he has won three and is taking blow after blow against teams playing in the second half of the table. For the moment, there is no reaction from the group because you are missing a lot of things”he declared on the microphone of RMC.

Every senior with an Apple Watch should set up these 5 health alerts

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

From emergency help to medication reminders, your Apple Watch can support you as you age.

Apple Watches are owned by people of all ages, so wearing one doesn't mark you as vulnerable, unlike a medical pendant. This is important to some older people who do not see themselves as "seniors", as explained by the National Institute of Aging. Older individuals know age does not stop them from living independent lives, and an Apple Watch can help them stay healthy when they do.

The idea that technology can help older people live independently for longer is hugely attractive to independent-minded boomers. Stand and movement reminders, fitness tracking and the step counter help seniors stay more active, keeping them healthier for longer. As Apple invests in healthcare research, the smartwatch gets better at detecting short term illness, boosting self-care.

With permission, the Find My app helps younger people take better care of their elderly relatives by tracking their location. There are also a number of health alerts every senior with an Apple Watch should set up.

Apple Watch has great tools for emergency help, which can be especially beneficial for seniors. These include Emergency SOS, Medical ID and Emergency Contacts.

Emergency SOS contacts emergency services when things go wrong. To use this feature, press and hold the side button, then drag the Emergency Call slider or keep holding the button until the call is made. This needs a cellular watch with an active plan, Wi-Fi calling or the paired iPhone nearby.

To turn on Emergency SOS, open the Watch app on your iPhone, tap Emergency SOS and turn on Hold Side Button to Dial.

Access to Medical ID and Emergency Contacts can be found in the Health app on your iPhone. Tap your profile picture and select Medical ID. Tap Edit, enter your details and add trusted Emergency Contacts. Medics who find your Apple Watch will review your Medical ID, so keep your allergies or conditions up to date to help medics deliver the treatment you need.

Over one in four people aged 65 or older fall unexpectedly each year, says the US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). In addition, 319,000 older people are hospitalized for hip fractures after a fall every year. Pew Research claims that, in 2023, 26% of US adults aged 65 or older lived alone, which compounds the potential risk of falling. Fall Detection may protect them.

Fall Detection is enabled automatically if you tell the Health app you are 55 or older. Once activated, it will call emergency services if the built-in sensors on your Apple Watch detect you are immobile after taking a fall.

If you fall, an alert message appears on your Apple Watch display, asking if you fell. If you request help, do not answer or remain immobile, the system will call emergency services. To turn on Fall Detection, open Emergency SOS on your iPhone and toggle feature On. On Apple Watch, open Settings > SOS, toggle Fall Detection to On, Select Always On and confirm.

Crash Detection does the same after a car crash and is available on recent Apple Watch models. To turn on Crash Detection, go to Emergency SOS and toggle Call After Serious Crash to On.

CDC data shows that a person dies of cardiovascular disease every 34 seconds in the US. While an Apple Watch cannot identify heart attacks, it can help monitor overall heart health including continuous heart rate monitoring and help to identify atrial fibrillation.

An Apple Watch uses green and infrared LED lights on its back to continuously measure your heart rate. This feature works automatically. Another feature lets you take an accurate ECG (electrocardiogram) reading to help detect irregular heart rates or the signs of atrial fibrillation. To use the ECG, open the ECG app, hold your wrist steady and touch the Digital Crown with your finger — you'll get a reading in thirty seconds.

The watch will also warn you if your heart goes above or below set thresholds, such as above 120bpm or below 40bpm. Apple Watch shares this information with the Health app on your iPhone, where you will find a VO2 Max reading that assesses your overall cardio fitness based on the information gathered by the sensors on your watch.

To enable Heart-related features on your Apple Watch, open Settings > Heart and toggle the available heart features you want to use On.

Forget to take your pills? Medication Reminders can help you stay up-to-date. To set this up, open the Health app on your iPhone, tap Browse > Medications and then Add Medication. Search for your medicine, tap Add, follow the prompts for setting up a schedule and tap Done.

You can add multiple medications, after which your Apple Watch will tap your wrist and send you a notification when it's time to take your dose. You can create a log of medicinal compliance to share with a doctor. Bonus: The sleep tracker on Apple Watch can flag potential sleep apnea for medical investigation. To set it up, open Sleep on Apple Watch (or in the Health app on your iPhone). Turn on Track Sleep with Apple Watch. In the Health app, tap Browse > Respiratory > Sleep Apnea Notifications.

Apple believes everyone should be able to use its technology and includes accessibility features in its products for no extra charge. That commitment extends to the Apple Watch, which is packed with tools to help people live independent lives.

Hearing loss becomes more common with age, with Achieve claiming one in four US adults in their sixties suffer from it. Apple's Sound Recognition tool helps by using the Apple Watch and iPhone to listen for specific sounds, such as doorbells, sirens or fire alarms.

Enable Sound Recognition in Accessibility Settings on your iPhone. Tap Sound & Name Recognition > Sound Recognition, toggle to On and choose the sounds you want it to recognize.

Apple has also figured out how to make Apple Watch more usable by those who are visually impaired. Press the side button to open Control Center, tap the AA icon and turn the Digital Crown to adjust font size to make it easier to read. If you want more information on any of these features, Apple provides an extensive explanation here.

French Judo Legend Teddy Riner Announces 2027 Presidential Bid, Drawing Praise From Emmanuel Macron

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

French judo superstar Teddy Riner, a five-time Olympic gold medalist and multiple world champion, has announced that he is running for president of France in 2027 in a fiery speech posted on social media.

The athlete, who has never held political office but is one of France’s most recognizable and popular sporting figures, portrayed himself as a political outsider determined to stand for ordinary French citizens which he feels have been let down. Related Stories XYZ Films, CAA Media Finance Ink U.S. Deal With Samuel Goldwyn Films for Dark Comedy 'Kill Me' Starring Charlie Day and Allison Williams (EXCLUSIVE)

Riner’s announcement comes at a particularly turbulent moment in France, just five months before a presidential election that has exposed deep divisions across the political spectrum.

The far-right party Rassemblement National is dominating the polls by a wide margin, with Marine Le Pen drawing between 33% and 37% of first-round voting intentions in recent surveys, roughly twice the support of her closest rivals. Jean-Luc Mélenchon, who leads the radical-left party La France Insoumise, has been gaining ground and is polling at around 17% to 19%, while the political center remains fragmented, with no candidate emerging to succeed Emmanuel Macron. The country has also been rocked by weeks of mass protests over the soaring cost of living and conditions in schools which escalated into violent riots.

“My name is Teddy Riner, and today I’m telling you straight to your face: I am a candidate in the 2027 presidential election,” he said while facing the camera and sitting at a desk in a presidential-style setting while bookshelves and a tricolor French flag in the background.

La résignation n'est pas une option. Face aux difficultés de notre pays, l'heure est venue de prendre ses responsabilités.⁰⁰Je suis candidat à l'élection présidentielle de 2027. ⁰⁰#TeddyRiner2027 #Présidentielle2027 #LaFrancequonveut pic.twitter.com/nM7rTfyTrH

“I know what people are going to say about me: ‘He’s crazy,’ ‘He’s not part of the establishment,’ ‘He doesn’t know the rules,’ ‘He’s never been involved in politics.’ But ask yourselves this question: What is really crazy today?”

Riner went on to paint a bleak picture of France, citing growing public debt, economic hardship and a breakdown of law and order.

“Is it crazy to refuse to watch France collapse under the weight of its debt, a little more every day? To see a country so rich pretend that it is poor?” he asked.

Riner criticized what he described as an unfair system that places an excessive burden on working people.

“Is it crazy to reject the hypocrisy of a system where those who get up early, work hard and follow the rules have no choice but to pay for everyone else?”

Macron reacted warmly to Riner’s announcement in an interview with the French broadcaster France 5 welcoming his decision to enter politics and praising his sense of duty. “Teddy Riner is a tremendous athlete who makes our country proud,” Macron said. “I supported him in competitions and as an athlete, and he is a committed man.”

Macron, who cannot seek a third consecutive presidential term in 2027, applauded the prospect of citizens from outside the political establishment taking part in the national debate.

“Political life, the life of the nation, does not belong to professionals,” Macron said, alluding to his own path, as he came into politics a decade ago after working in investment banking at Rothschild. “The life of the nation belongs to women and men who want to give their time, who are ready to commit themselves and fight difficult battles to make our country better. I am very happy that someone like Teddy Riner, who is a great champion, has decided to take part in this presidential debate and bring his convictions, his ideas and his talent,“ Macron continued.

Anthropic reportedly plans to move forward with IPO plans

Johnny Depp & Ti West Land In A Chilly London For ‘Ebenezer’ Global Premiere

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Christmas came early to London’s Leicester Square on Monday as Johnny Depp touched down for a red-carpet global premiere of Ebenezer in the company of director Ti West and cast including Rupert Grint, Sam Claflin, Tramell Tillman, Ellie Bamber and Robert Strong.

The red carpet was decked out with Christmas trees and artificial snow while 1950s festive classics blasted out in the backdrop, and if by magic, temperatures dropped, ending an unseasonably balmy autumn.

The chilly conditions did not deter fans who crammed into the red-carpet enclosure, most of them there for a glimpse of Depp. He did not disappoint, arriving in a timely fashion to talk to handpicked media and then walk the fan line. Related Stories News Johnny Depp Open To Reprising 'Pirates Of The Caribbean' Role To "Send Them Off On A Better Note" Documentary Joe Perry Bows Out Of Hollywood Vampires Doc Screening As Aerosmith Ramps Up For Hollywood Bowl Gigs

Depp’s latest role as Ebenezer Scrooge is being billed as the actor’s comeback, after being left out in cold following his high-profile legal battles with ex-wife Amber Heard. Watch on Deadline

Rumors are rife that if Ebenezer performs well, it could help green-light a reboot of the Pirates of the Caribbean franchise with Depp reprising his Captain Jack Sparrow role.

Whether this comes to pass remains to be seen but it was plain to see on Monday night that Depp was the main draw, with the public at least appearing to have moved on.

Talking to Deadline on the red carpet, West said he and Depp had bonded over a shared love of Charles Dickens’ ‘A Christmas Carol’ as well as admiration for Alastair Sim’s performance as Ebenezer Scrooge in an 1951 adaptation.

´Ebenezer’ director Ti West talks on casting Johnny Depp as Ebenezer Scrooge: “He [Depp] was obsessed with Scrooge since he was a little kid. When he found out about the movie, he was like, ‘Alistair Sims is a huge reason why I’m even an actor.’” pic.twitter.com/A2zFDV8J5r

“I’ve been obsessed with it since I was little kid. I would read it every Christmas, and I was into it because it was a ghost story and then I saw the George C. Scott film when I was really little and Marley really scared me,” he said referring to Clive Donner’s 1984 version.

“I’ve had a fascination of it and a version in my head that I was waiting for someone to make that they didn’t make. What’s also cool about it is that it’s one of the rare stories that because it’s so enduring, redoing it, is part of the charm,” he said.

West said Depp had approached him about the role when he heard the production was in the works.

“He was obsessed with Scrooge since when he was a little kid also which I didn’t know and so when he found about the movie he was like, ‘Alastair Sim is a huge reason why I am even an actor.’ It was a weird cosmic thing where it was meant to be,” said West.

Depp confirmed West’s account in a public interview ahead of heading inside the cinema.

“I adored it as a child and to this day I know there is no better person to play Ebenezer Scrooge than Alastair Sim… the challenge is to find another angle,” said Depp, praising West for creating a atmosphere on set in which there was space to experiment with the role.

Johnny Depp talks childhood obsession with ‘A Christmas Carol’ and Ebenezer Scrooge at the ‘Ebenezer’ premiere in London pic.twitter.com/hnRAOiODqS

Not surprisingly, given West’s horror filmography, there is a gothic horror to feel to the adaptation, giving the work an extra frisson that goes beyond past big screen versions.

Ellie Bamber, who plays Ebenezer’s long lost love, that it also remains very much a family film. “It’s still very Christmassy with a hint of fright.”

‘Ebenezer’ star Ellie Bamber discusses Ti West’s gothic horror take on Charles Dickens classic: “It’s still very Christmassy with a hint of fright.” pic.twitter.com/EzT3xoTHKa

Electrical technique concentrates milk without using heat, challenging traditional preservation methods

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Food  |  Read Source →

Dehydrating milk to turn it into powder is, today, an energy-intensive process that subjects the product to high temperatures. Powdered milk is widely used by the food industry and allows the product to be stored and transported in a stable condition for long periods of time without the need for refrigeration. While industrial methods are effective, they have high energy costs and can alter heat-sensitive components, such as whey proteins, enzymes or vitamins.

Klint Kubiak gives defensive coordinator Rob Leonard a vote of confidence

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

The Raiders defense allowed 38 points in the loss to the 49ers on Sunday, the sixth consecutive game of giving up 30-plus points. But coach Klint Kubiak said Monday that he does not anticipate changes to the defense, giving defensive coordinator Rob Leonard a vote of confidence.

“In fact, I see us doing the things we said we’re going to do [but] better,” Kubiak said, via Ryan McFadden of ESPN. “Stand behind, Rob. Stand behind our defensive staff. Stand behind our players. And I think the world wants someone’s head on the platter every time things don’t go right. But the way that I see it is that we brought people here for a reason: to get a job done and develop players for the long haul.”

The Raiders ranked eighth in points allowed (18 ppg) and 11th in yards (316.8 ypg) in the team’s 3-0 start. Since Week 4, Las Vegas is last in points allowed (35.7 ppg), rushing yards allowed (168.7) and defensive efficiency (17.54) and 29th in sacks (nine).

The Raiders have lost five of their past six games.

“I have seen it work. Just not for four quarters,” Kubiak said of his defense. “As a group, it’s putting four quarters of football together no matter who’s the opponent. You play a damn good offense in San Francisco; there’s got to be a happy medium somewhere between the first half and second half.”

Why Do I Go Numb Instead of Feeling Things?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 9, 2026 | Reviewed by Davia Sills

I was an actor in New York. Yes, that’s right—I didn’t emerge from the womb asking people how they felt for a living. I had dreams of being on Broadway, and after years of work, I finally got invited to the final callbacks for the lead role in a Broadway musical. For this final audition, I was going to wear my grandfather’s vintage pinstriped suit. I planned on changing into it at the audition studio to make sure it looked crisp. But while walking down 43rd Street, half a block from the audition, the suit fell out of my hands and into a pile of Central Park carriage horse manure. Terrified that I’d smell up the room and ruin my chances at the role, I rushed to the bathroom and tried to scrub out the stains before putting the suit on and walking into the audition.

Suffice it to say, I didn’t get the part. I got some generic feedback about how I didn’t have the right look. But I don’t really remember much of that part—the days and weeks afterward are a blank spot in my memory. And while I can recall the moment I dropped that suit on 43rd Street like it was yesterday, I can’t remember how I felt about not landing my big Broadway break. I can barely remember finding out I didn’t get it. The whole experience is a fog, as if the details and the feelings attached to them have been swallowed up together. When I reach back and try to remember how I felt about losing out on this role, the only thing I feel is... nothing.

Emotional numbing is a sense of being disconnected from your feelings. You might have a sense that something matters to you but not be able to feel much about it. Sometimes we disconnect like this in response to stress that threatens to overwhelm us. If we feel less, we can keep moving forward.

I see this often in work with clients—when moving towards a memory that holds a lot of charge, they’ll often just go blank, fuzz out, or suddenly be unable to recall details. I think of it like the villain from that ’80s movie, The NeverEnding Story: the Nothing. If you’re not an ’80s kid like me, the Nothing threatens to consume the magical land of Fantasia. It grows as people lose their hopes and forget their dreams. That’s the image that comes up for me when I try to describe my experience of it. I start to feel the big feeling coming on. Sometimes I even experience it for a moment. But as it threatens to overwhelm me, the Nothing swoops in so that I can move on.

While I invented my own ’80s fanboy name for what I’m experiencing, researchers have been exploring this topic for some time. There’s a related experience called alexithymia, which is difficulty identifying and describing our own emotions. Imagine being asked how you feel about a certain topic, reaching into wherever emotions live in us, and just hitting a wall. From the inside, it can be really hard to distinguish between “I don’t know how I feel” and “I don’t feel anything.”

Emotional numbness isn’t unique to men, but we do seem to have a corner on the market. And that makes sense given the messages we receive about what is and isn’t acceptable to feel. Psychologist Ronald Levant uses the term normative male alexithymia to describe a pattern found in many men who experience difficulty identifying and expressing emotion—particularly those emotions tied to vulnerability or intimacy. He believes this pattern is rooted in the way men are socialized in our society.

Those of us raised as men learn these messages early and often. We inherit them and gift them to each other: “Don’t cry. Don’t be so sensitive. Don’t let them see you care.” I honestly can’t remember where I first received this message. I’m tempted to chalk that one up to the Nothing, too.

But my Nothing isn’t a villain from a kids movie. I think of him as a part of me that learned to perform a job. And he performs it very well. I suspect the Nothing is the reason I’ve been able to succeed at grad school, to switch careers, to build a private practice. But the Nothing doesn’t discriminate between the pain of the story and the importance of the story itself: A friendship ends, and we convince ourselves it wasn’t that important anyway. A dream falls to manure (literally), and we find something else to want.

In my experience, the Nothing doesn’t just take away the disappointment. It also makes it harder to remember how much we cared. I wish that I could have given myself the time to really feel the disappointment and the sadness of seeing my dream slip through my fingers. My experience feels incomplete in my mind, like a book without the final chapter.

Sometimes in the middle of a session, a client goes blank. It’s not like they fully check out of the room, but they’ll be dipping their toe into an emotion and then just lose connection with it. When that happens, I’ll often invite them to notice what happens as they get closer to it. And sometimes they can’t. Sometimes the details are fuzzy, or there’s simply nothing there, and they get frustrated. That’s okay.

Noticing we can’t connect is a good place to start. Sometimes that blankness is doing a job, too. Rather than forcing our way through it, we can get curious about what feeling it might be protecting. And maybe, just maybe, that curiosity gives way to a name: sorrow, regret, grief. The point isn’t to choose the emotion we think we’re supposed to have. It’s to explore if we can connect with even the faintest sense of “Yes, that’s it.”

I think about younger me walking down 43rd Street with my grandfather’s suit, so excited and scared about what might happen next. I wish I could go back and tell him: “I see how much this matters to you. Whether you get the role or not, I want you to remember how much this matters. Don’t let that be taken from you.” We don’t have to convince ourselves a dream didn’t matter just because it didn’t come true. Sometimes, nearly 20 years later, we can give ourselves the chance to write about it and remember that it mattered.

Levant, R. F., & Parent, M. C. (2019). The development and evaluation of a brief form of the Normative Male Alexithymia Scale (NMAS-BF). Journal of Counseling Psychology, 66(2), 224–233. doi:10.1037/cou0000312

Jaron Vesely, MSW, is a Registered Associate Clinical Social Worker (ASW #139998) and psychotherapist in Oakland, CA, practicing under the supervision of Emily Haydock, LMFT, at Juniper Sky. He explores the patterns we build to cope, connect, and make our way through this world.

Iran war helps fuel global drop in fossil fuel emissions — but there’s a catch

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

In the run-up to the COP31 U.N. climate change conference, scientists have revealed that global carbon emissions from fossil fuels are projected to drop by 0.4% this year, driven by growth in installed wind and solar power capacity, as well as oil price hikes caused by the Iran conflict. But climate goals are still unlikely to be met.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

The world's carbon emissions from oil, gas and coal are projected to decrease by 0.4% this year, driven partly by the expansion of solar and wind power capacity and the price increases caused by the Iran conflict, according to a new report.

The 2026 Global Carbon Budget report was published in the journal Earth System Science Data Nov. 10 and is due to be presented at the U.N. Climate Change Conference's COP31 meeting in Antalya, Turkey, this week.

Global carbon emissions have increased steadily since record-keeping began. This year is projected to have a slight decrease in fossil-fuel emissions, but that is partly due to price increases caused by the Iran conflict.

It is the first time annual carbon dioxide (CO2) emissions from fossil fuels have fallen since the coronavirus pandemic in 2020. But the overall emissions picture suggests the world is still likely to miss key climate goals.

"It is an encouraging sign that the growth of emissions is slowing and decarbonisation efforts are starting to make a difference," said Joeri Rogelj, a climate scientist at the Centre for Environmental Policy at Imperial College London who wasn't involved in the research. "However, at 0.4%, the projected decline is very small," Rogelj told Live Science in an email.

The report projects 38.2 billion metric tons (42.1 billion U.S. tons) of emissions of the climate-warming greenhouse gas CO2 from fossil fuel sources this year. Greenhouse gases trap heat by absorbing the sun's radiation, and as their concentrations in the atmosphere increase, so do global average temperatures, leading to altered weather patterns and higher sea levels, as well as other major impacts.

The war with Iran has resulted in restricted fuel availability and high prices, with a corresponding reduction in demand

Part of the 0.4% reduction in CO2 emissions is attributable to 40 countries that have cut emissions over the past decade while growing their economies. These include the U.S., Australia, New Zealand, South Korea, the United Kingdom, Japan and about 30 countries in the European Union, the researchers reported.

When it comes to some of the biggest emitters, annual CO2 emissions are projected to decrease by 0.9% in China, 1.3% in the U.S., 1.5% in the European Union and 1% in Japan, but India's annual CO2 emissions are projected to grow by 4.4%.

Get the world’s most fascinating discoveries delivered straight to your inbox.

Carbon emissions are projected to decrease slightly in the U.S., China and the E.U. but to increase in India.

Fossil-fuel-linked CO2 emissions from the rest of the world are projected to decrease by 0.7%, driven mainly by a 1.9% drop in oil emissions. The report authors suggested that this is a short-term response to the global supply constraints and increased prices triggered by the Iran conflict.

"The war with Iran has resulted in restricted fuel availability and high prices, with a corresponding reduction in demand," Rogelj said.

While fossil fuel emissions and demand for oil are declining globally, overall demand for energy is actually increasing.

"Based only on the demand for energy, which has grown, we would have expected an increase in fossil CO2 emissions by about 0.8 gigatonnes," said study co-author Judith Hauck, a biogeoscientist at the Alfred Wegener Institute, Helmholtz Centre for Polar and Marine Research in Germany.

About two-thirds of that increase in demand is being met by solar and wind power and electric vehicles, showing that clean technologies are making a real difference in reducing global emissions, Hauck said. However, the hike in energy prices that resulted from the Iran conflict also decreased demand by 0.4%, she said.

"So only together with the energy crisis did we bring the change in emissions down to negative," Hauck told Live Science, adding that she expects CO2 emissions to rebound if the conflict ends, as they did after the temporary emissions drop caused by the lockdowns during the COVID-19 pandemic.

The exceptional circumstances that energy markets experienced this year make it clear that the small projected emissions decline is no reason to declare that emissions are now reducing, but there are hopeful signs, Rogelj said.

"Despite my muted enthusiasm about whether these lower emissions for 2026 represent the start of a long-term decline, the analysis shows encouraging signs of deeper long-term structural changes," he said. "But this shift must accelerate to ensure it is not outpaced by energy demand growth."

The report found that clean energy growth isn't yet fast enough to drive a steady decline in global CO2 emissions attributable to fossil fuels. The study authors expect that to happen in the next few years, Hauck said, as long as there is a continued push to deploy solar and wind power, batteries and electric vehicles.

CO2 emissions from fossil fuels are just part of the picture, though. Because of increases in human-caused peat fires, deforestation and forest fires linked to dry El Niño conditions, total global CO2 emissions from all sources this year are projected to increase slightly from 42.8 billion metric tons (47.2 billion U.S. tons) last year to reach a record 43.3 billion metric tons (47.7 billion U.S. tons) this year, according to the report.

This means that the total budget of CO2 that can be emitted before global warming reaches 1.5 degrees Celsius (2.7 degrees Fahrenheit) above preindustrial levels is now just 150 billion metric tons (165 billion U.S. tons) ‪—‬ equivalent to less than four years of emissions at the current rate.

Hauck, like many climate scientists, thinks we have already missed our chance to hit the 1.5 C target and could only get back to that level through extensive removal of CO2 from the atmosphere.?

"I'm still hopeful for something between 1.5 and 2 C [3.6 F]," Hauck said. "But for 1.5 degrees, we would need to see such a drastic strong drop in emissions. Based on what we've seen in the past years, even with this strong increase in avoided emissions, I don't think we can make that."

To halt global warming from increasing ever further, the world needs to be more ambitious in its planned emissions cuts, said Rogeli. We need to bring annual emissions down to zero to protect future generations from a double whammy of “having to try to transform economies so that they produce less climate pollution, while dealing with the impacts and damages of an increasingly adverse climate system," he said.

Chris Simms is a freelance journalist who previously worked at New Scientist for more than 10 years, in roles including chief subeditor and assistant news editor. He was also a senior subeditor at Nature and has a degree in zoology from Queen Mary University of London. In recent years, he has written numerous articles for New Scientist and in 2018 was shortlisted for Best Newcomer at the Association of British Science Writers awards.

Game Recap: Saints host Browns in Week 9 clash

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

The New Orleans Saints were back in action after the bye week when the Cleveland Browns visited the Big Easy on Sunday afternoon. Here's a full recap of the matchup.

Cleveland began the game by going three-and-out, before Audric Estime got the Saints offense going straight away with three runs totalling 32 yards.

Tyler Shough then found Juwan Johnson for 19 more with his first throw, but a few unsuccessful runs and a sack later, the Saints were held to a field goal attempt.

Charlie Smyth converted from 30 yards to give the black and gold an early lead.

The Browns were then forced into another quick punt as the Saints defense got off to a strong start.

Shough found Chris Olave for 11 yards before breaking off for a 14-yard run, but it was soon another field goal attempt after he was sacked on 3rd down.

This time, Smyth was no good from 52 yards, and the score remained 3-0.

Shadeur Sanders found the first big play of the game for Cleveland with a 16-yard pass to Dylan Sampson, who also had a few short gains on the ground as the Browns drove past midfield.

After being stuffed short of the chains on 3rd down, a 41-yard field goal from Andre Szmyt tied the game.

Shough then copied a play from Paris with a 52-yard bomb downfield to Barion Brown as the Saints sought to regain the lead.

But a play later, Jordyn Tyson fumbled after making a catch, which the defense fell on to return possession to the Browns.

New Orleans was able to force another quick punt out of the Browns, but Brown fumbled on the return. Cleveland fell on it in the red zone.

A penalty against Carl Granderson helped the visitors set up 1st-and-goal, but he made up for it by sacking Sanders to push them back.

A few plays later, Sanders was just short of the goal line on 3rd-and-goal. The Browns offense stayed out there, and the Saints were able to come out big with a stop on 4th down to keep the game at 3-3.

Shough connected with Olave for 36 yards to move the Saints out from deep in their own territory, but after another pass for just one yard and two incompletions, they were forced to punt just before the half.

New Orleans went three-and-out and punted to begin the second half, before Sanders scrambled for 11 yards on 3rd down to avoid the same fate.

He then completed a 60-yard pass to Denzel Boston to set up 1st-and-goal.

But the Saints' defense came up huge once again, with a tackle-for-loss, sack, and incompletion from Sanders limiting Cleveland to a chip-shot field goal that gave them a 6-3 lead.

Shough responded with a scoring drive of his own, on a 54-yard drive highlighted by a 12-yard pass to Noah Fant and his own scrambles moving the ball down the field.

Smyth's 27-yard kick tied the game at 6-6.

Cleveland then went three-and-out as Granderson got to the quarterback once again, but New Orleans would do the same, which gave the visitors the ball to begin the fourth.

Sanders got the ball to midfield with a 10-yard pass to KC Concepcion, but failed to find another first down. He threw an incomplete pass to Sampson on 4th-and-2, and the Saints took over on downs again.

Taking advantage of the shorter field, Shough moved the black and gold into field goal range with a 9-yard throw to Tyson.

After being sacked by Myles Harden, the Saints were forced into yet another field goal, which Smyth converted from 48 yards.

But the lead didn't last long, as Sanders threw a 64-yard touchdown to Concepcion on the opening play of the ensuing drive to give Cleveland a 13-9 advantage.

New Orleans then went three-and-out, before Cleveland had another punt of their own around the two-minute warning.

Needing a touchdown, Shough found Olave for 33 yards to move the drive into the red zone. He then found Alvin Kamara for 8 more, before Estime ran in the 2-yard score to re-establish the lead for the black and gold.

But Sanders threw twice for a total of 37 yards to Jerry Jeudy, which set up a field goal that Szmyt converted from 51 yards to force overtime.

The Saints received first, and Kamara broke off three big runs as the ball moved past midfield.

Shough then found Devaughn Vele for 9 yards before Estime ran for 9 more.

Shough ran for 8 yards before finding Vele again, setting up a 15-yard touchdown throw to Johnson to give the black and gold a 23-16 lead.

Needing a touchdown of their own, two passes from Sanders to Boston helped Cleveland into Saints territory.

Sanders then found Harold Fannin Jr for a 39-yard touchdown.

Cleveland opted to go for two and the win, which is where the Saints came up with the biggest stop of the day as Sanders' pass intended for Boston fell incomplete.

New Orleans held on 23-22 to set up a huge divisional matchup next weekend with the Carolina Panthers.

Elon Musk Grok AI Predicts a Wild DOGE Price: Is Dogecoin About to Explode?

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Dogecoin (DOGE USD price) is currently trading in a quiet, almost forgotten range near multi-month lows. However, Elon Musk’s Grok AI predicts that one wildly ambitious catalyst could still send it into the stratosphere before 2027.

Imagine this scenario: In a highly unlikely but not entirely impossible turn of events, Elon Musk and the X platform announce a comprehensive integration of Dogecoin as the native currency for tipping, subscriptions, creator payouts, advertising settlements, and even certain X Premium and xAI-related services.

At the same time, Tesla expands its acceptance of Dogecoin beyond merchandise, allowing it to be used for a significant portion of vehicle and energy product payments in select markets. Meanwhile, SpaceX begins accepting Dogecoin for certain commercial satellite and rideshare contracts.

Together, this creates a self-reinforcing loop of real utility, viral cultural momentum, and continuous buying pressure that the market has never experienced from a meme coin before. The retail FOMO collides with genuine transactional demand, and the narrative of “the people’s currency” finally achieves scale.

DOGE is currently hovering around the $0.069–$0.071 area after a prolonged period of lower highs and declining volume. The chart shows a multi-month downtrend that has compressed into a relatively tight range near long-term support.

Historically, Dogecoin’s largest moves have occurred when it breaks out of extended basing periods on expanding volume and renewed social momentum.

A decisive weekly close above the $0.085–$0.095 resistance zone would mark the first meaningful higher high in some time and open the door to a retest of the $0.12–$0.15 region.

In a true parabolic scenario driven by a major real-world catalyst, measured-move extensions from the current base and prior cycle Fibonacci projections could theoretically support far more aggressive upside once the market shifts from indifference to euphoria.

Momentum indicators remain subdued, leaving substantial room for a sharp expansion if buying pressure returns. While the current price action reflects skepticism and fatigue, Dogecoin has repeatedly shown that narrative and celebrity-driven catalysts can overwhelm technical resistance when they arrive with sufficient force.

The combination of cultural staying power and the possibility of meaningful utility remains its most powerful (if unpredictable) asset.

While Grok AI predicts a parabolic move for DOGE, Bitcoin Hyper (HYPER) is more reliably focused on another significant opportunity: bringing high-speed financial applications to the Bitcoin ecosystem.

Bitcoin Hyper aims to leverage BTC’s vast liquidity through a Layer 2 network designed to enable faster transactions and decentralized finance (DeFi) functionality.

Its architecture uses the Solana Virtual Machine (SVM), bringing Solana-style execution capabilities to the Bitcoin-focused ecosystem.

Bitcoin Hyper’s goal is clear: to merge Bitcoin’s substantial liquidity and established investor base with transaction speeds tailored for modern decentralized applications. Key features include near-instant transaction finality and low costs, which are central to its positioning.

HYPER is attracting attention through its presale, which has reportedly raised over $33M, demonstrating strong early fundraising momentum.

It offers a 35% annual percentage yield (APY) for staking rewards, providing another incentive for early participants. Token holders can stake their HYPER tokens to accumulate additional rewards instead of leaving their allocation idle.

With the planned launch date of November 26 approaching, the presale is entering a crucial phase. Those interested in Bitcoin’s expanding Layer 2 ecosystem are looking forward to Hyper’s market debut before it lists on centralized exchanges (CEXs).

AI-Powered Precision Brain Stimulation Reduces PTSD in Weeks

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

After years or decades, problems and patterns can become deeply entrenched. Be a fly on the wall during a sample couples therapy session and witness the slow, deliberate work of changing those patterns for a more enduring love.

Self Tests are all about you. Are you outgoing or introverted? Are you a narcissist? Does perfectionism hold you back? Find out the answers to these questions and more with Psychology Today.

Posted November 9, 2026 | Reviewed by Davia Sills

Neuroscience is at the start of a tsunami of innovation thanks to artificial intelligence (AI). Machine learning can identify meaningful biological indicators from reams of noisy brain activity data to offer more personalized, targeted treatment. A new study in the November-December 2026 edition of Personalized Medicine in Psychiatry shows how the combination of AI with noninvasive transcranial magnetic stimulation (TMS) can reduce post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD) in just two weeks.

“These findings mark a significant step forward in PTSD therapy, offering a non-pharmacologic treatment for individuals who have not benefited from traditional therapeutic approaches,” wrote the research team of Alexander Taghva, Jeffery Lindquist, and Anthony Kim.

An estimated 70 percent of the world’s population will experience a traumatic event during their lifetime, yet only 5.6 percent will develop post-traumatic stress disorder (PTSD) from the experience, according to the World Health Organization. In the U.S. alone, an estimated 7.7 million adult Americans suffer from PTSD, according to the National Institute of Mental Health (NIMH).

Post-traumatic stress disorder is a mental health condition where symptoms may emerge for up to three months after a stressful or traumatic event, persist for more than a month, and interfere with performing daily life activities.

Adults with PTSD have at least one symptom of re-experiencing and avoidance, in addition to at least two symptoms related to problems with arousal and reactivity, as well as cognition and mood issues, per NIMH. Symptoms of re-experiencing may include flashbacks, physical signs of stress, distressing thoughts, and recurring memories or dreams. Evading feelings or thoughts associated with the trauma, or staying away from anything that might be a reminder of the traumatic event, are symptoms of avoidance. Issues with arousal and reactivity may include irritability, outbursts, risky behavior, insomnia, concentration problems, tenseness, or skittishness. PTSD symptoms that affect cognition and mood include difficulty feeling positive, feeling socially isolated, loss of interest in normal activities, persistent negative thoughts and emotions, exaggerated feelings of blame, and trouble recalling crucial elements of traumatic events.

Factors that may increase the potential for PTSD include prior traumatic experiences, especially childhood trauma, a history of mental illness or mental illness in the family, post-event stressors such as the death of a loved one, injury, or job loss, lack of social support post-trauma, and feelings of extreme fear, helplessness, or horror, according to the National Institute of Mental Health.

PTSD treatment options include psychotherapy such as cognitive behavioral therapy (CBT), cognitive processing therapy (CPT), prolonged exposure, eye movement desensitization and reprocessing (EMDR) therapy, narrative exposure therapy (NET), and medications, according to the American Psychological Association (APA). Common PTSD antidepressants, per the APA, include the selective serotonin reuptake inhibitors (SSRIs) sertraline (generic name for Zoloft), paroxetine (generic name for Paxil), and fluoxetine (generic name for Prozac), and serotonin and norepinephrine reuptake inhibitors (SNRIs) like venlafaxine (generic name for Effexor).

“The results of this study are particularly meaningful in the context of PTSD treatment, where a substantial fraction of patients does not fully respond to existing pharmacologic and psychological therapies,” wrote the study authors.

For this study, the researchers focused on PTSD among the veterans. The study participants included over 70 military veterans between the ages of 18 and 55 years who were diagnosed with PTSD, of whom 36 were randomly selected to receive non-invasive brain stimulation treatment and 37 received placebo treatment.

“The prevalence of combat-related PTSD among U.S. military veterans is estimated to be 7 percent, with prevalence increased among female service members (13 percent) in comparison to males (6 percent),” the researchers wrote.

First, a baseline electroencephalogram (EEG) was recorded noninvasively via a sensor cap for 10 minutes at the same time as an electrocardiogram (EKG). The treatment frequency was determined via an AI decision tree algorithm output based on the 10-minute EEG data recordings as input. Recordings were done at the start and end of the therapy. For a period of two weeks, study participants received 10 real or placebo treatments. Those receiving the actual treatment received personalized neuromodulation, where repetitive transcranial magnetic stimulation (rTMS) was customized to the participant’s resting-state EEG alpha rhythm.

Brain waves are patterns of neuronal electrical activity that are a continuous series of peaks and troughs that occur between neurons via electrical pulses and are measured in Hertz (Hz). EEG measures brain waves via their electrical activity. Depending on the frequencies and amplitude, brain waves are categorized as alpha (8-12 Hz), beta (13-30 Hz), theta (4-10 Hz), delta (0.5-1.5 Hz), or gamma (30-120 Hz) oscillations.

In 1924, German psychiatrist Hans Berger (1873-1941) was the first to record human electrical brain signals using EEG. Berger observed a 10-cycles-per-second rhythm and named it “alpha.”

Alpha brain waves happen during a state of calmness and relaxation that occurs when meditating lightly, sitting quietly with eyes closed, or daydreaming.

In this study, the researchers personalized the repetitive transcranial magnetic stimulation (rTMS) pulse rate to the individual participant’s resting-state EEG alpha (α) rhythm, a process called alpha repetitive transcranial magnetic stimulation (α-rTMS). The brain stimulation device used for the study was the FDA-cleared EEG-guided neuromodulation device for adults with PTSD called MeRT by Wave Neuroscience, based in Newport Beach, California.

The research team reported a 34 percent reduction in PTSD symptom severity and better sleep, with a 44 percent reduction in sleep disturbances as measured by the Pittsburgh Sleep Quality Index Addendum for PTSD (PSQI-A), which measures sleep problems in PTSD patients.

“The effectiveness of ⍺-rTMS could be attributed to its precision in targeting dysregulated alpha band activity, possibly related to the underlying default mode network (DMN), implicated in PTSD’s neuropathology,” the researchers posited. “This precision heralds a novel, more personalized approach to neuromodulation therapies, potentially increasing efficacy, to effectively address certain PTSD-related symptoms.”

Humble Hauler autonomous trailer takes the truck out of trucking [update]

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Less than a year after its founding, truckless trucking brand Humble Robotics has secured more than $75 million after a $51 million Series A funding round that will fuel the company’s transition from prototype to preproduction.

The latest funding round for the driverless, cab-less, and truck-less battery-electric freight platform developer was led by Obvious Ventures with participation from other existing investors Eclipse and Energy Impact Partners. Humble said it will use the funding for customer pilots, testing and validation, and early manufacturing.

“We’ve spent years proving that a truck can drive itself,” explains Humble Robotics founder and CEO, Eyal Cohen. “The harder question is how to make autonomy something a freight operator can depend on every day.”

We originally covered the Humble Hauler back in May. You can check out the original article, below, then let us know what you think of the company’s prospects in the comments.

The startup that dares to ask, “Why does the Macbook driving my freight around need a whole cab?” just showed off a fully driverless, cab-less, and truck-less battery-electric freight platform designed to handle containerized cargo more efficiently than ever.

Instead of looking at the tractor and asking how to make it work without a driver, Humble looked at the trailer, itself, redesigning the container chassis as a vehicle from the ground up. The end result isn’t just an autonomous solution, it’s an overall lighter platform without a cab that saves shippers the weight of a sleeper cab, a day cab, or the truck at all.

The Humble approach is said to improve payload efficiency of shipping containers, and intended for dock-to-dock operation.

“I have dedicated my career to building electric and autonomous vehicle technology,” explains Eyal Cohen, Humble Robotics’ founder and CEO. “For the first time, freight can be fully automated all the way to the loading dock. We are making freight sustainable, safe and efficient in a way no one thought was possible. And we’re doing it with an exceptional team of industry veterans and AV experts.”

Emerging from stealth after a $24 million funding round, Humble claims to have completed its first Humble Hauler prototype in less than six months – and they’ll soon be ready to begin autonomous testing in pilot programs with a number of logistics partners.

The vehicle uses a combination of cameras, lidar, and radar for 360-degree awareness (unhindered by seeing around a cab), and uses vision-language-action models to help the system respond to new scenarios.

Thanks to superior battery tech, a maturing global charging infrastructure, and a permanently damaged MidEast oil supply, there’s never been a better time to be an EV startup. Humble is hitting all the electric trailer buzzwords, too, while also touting the fact that the electric powertrain can reduce a company’s to volatile fuel prices even as it helps produce other cost benefits like lower maintenance costs.

There is a reason why the transportation industry developed separate cabs and trailers. Trailers can be loaded or unloaded for days and are much less expensive than trucks that serve both purposes in one piece. That allows the more expensive piece of the transport puzzle to be used more and allows the trailer to be loaded and unloaded while the cab is elsewhere working or in the shop. Query whether the savings they cite will surpass the costs of not having the flexibility of a cab and trailer design.

And if a robot like this can deliver on its promises without the baked-in inefficiencies of humanoids, they might be one step closer to provide that the most efficient solution to a problem is rarely a general one, and even rarer is it a solution that looks like a human.
